\mathcal{M}
and construct the path
p_{\mathcal{M}}^{2}=p_{\mathcal{M}}(A,Q_{i-1})\oplus\langle Q_{i-1},Q_{i+2}\rangle\oplus p_{\mathcal{M}}(Q_{i+2},B)
with two fewer non-colliders.

(iii)
(iii)(b)

Q_{k-2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}Q_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}Q_{k}
, and
Q_{k-2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}Q_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. This case is symmetric to
(i)
(i)(c)
and holds by an analogous argument.

(iii)
(iii)(c)

that is
Q_{k-2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}Q_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}Q_{k}
, and
Q_{k-2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}Q_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. This case is symmetric to
(i)
(i)(b)
and holds by an analogous argument.

D.1
Results Related
Wang et al. [2024b]
and
Wang et al. [2025]

D.1.1
R13
and
Wang et al. [2024b]

In this section, we show that our phrasing of
R13
leads to equivalent orientations as the phrasing of the rule originally given by
Wang et al. [2024b]
. We first state
Wang et al. [2024b]
’s version of the rule in Theorem
54
below (labeled
Wang-R13
), which used their concept of an unbridged path, also defined below. We note that
Wang et al. [2024b]
refer to the rule presented in Theorem
54
as R12.

Corollary
56
shows that
Wang-R13
and
R13
will lead to the same edge mark orientations when executed in combination with the remaining orientation rules. The proof of Corollary
56
relies on Corollary
55
which is based on
Wang et al. [2024b]
proof of Theorem
54
and Lemma
57
below.

Definition 53

(Unbridged path relative to
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}
, Definition 1 of
Wang et al., 2024b
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be a partial mixed graph and
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}\subset\mathbf{V}
. If there is an unshielded path
p=\langle V_{1},\dots,V_{k}\rangle
,
k>1
of the form
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
in
\mathcal{G}
,
\{V_{1},\dots,V_{k}\}\cap\mathbf{V}^{\prime}=\emptyset
and such that
\mathcal{F}_{1}\setminus\mathcal{F}_{2}\neq\emptyset,
and
\mathcal{F}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\neq\emptyset,
where
\mathcal{F}_{i}=\{V\in\mathbf{V}^{\prime}:V\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{i}\text{, or }V\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{i}\text{ is in $\mathcal{G}$}\},
then
p
is called an unbridged path relative to
\mathbf{V}^{\prime}.

Theorem 54

(Theorem 1 of
Wang et al., 2024b
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be a partial mixed graph.

Wang-R13

Suppose edge
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
is in
\mathcal{G}
and let
\mathbf{S}_{A}=\{V\in\mathbf{V}:V\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}A\text{ is in
}\mathcal{G}\}\cup\{A\}
. If there is an unbridged path
\langle V_{1},\dots,V_{k}\rangle,k>1,
relative to
\mathbf{S}_{A}
in
\mathcal{G}
such that for every
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
there is an unshielded path
p_{i}=\langle W_{i_{1}}=A,W_{i_{2}}=B,\dots,W_{i_{m}}=V_{i}\rangle,{{m}\geq 3}
with no edge
W_{i_{j}}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}W_{i_{j+1}}
,
j\in\{1,\dots,m-1\}
on
p_{i}
, then
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
is in every MAG represented by
\mathcal{G}
.

Corollary 55

(c.f. Proof of Theorem 1 of
Wang et al., 2024b
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be an essential ancestral graph and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=(\mathbf{V,E^{\prime}})
be an ancestral partial mixed graph such that
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have the same skeleton, the same set of minimal collider paths, and every invariant edge mark in
\mathcal{G}
is identical in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Suppose furthermore that edge orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
-
R4
,
R8
-
R12
. Let
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
be an edge in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and let
\mathbf{S}_{A}=\{V\in\mathbf{V}:V\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}A\text{ is in
}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\}\cup\{A\}
. Suppose that there is also an unbridged path
\langle V_{1},\dots,V_{k}\rangle,k>1,
relative to
\mathbf{S}_{A}
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
such that for every
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
there is an unshielded path
p_{i}=\langle W_{i_{1}}=A,W_{i_{2}}=B,\dots,W_{i_{m}}=V_{i}\rangle,{{m}\geq 3}
with no edge
W_{i_{j}}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}W_{i_{j+1}}
,
j\in\{1,\dots,m-1\}
on
p_{i}
.
Then there are nodes

C_{1},C_{2}\in\mathbf{S}_{A}
such that
C_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}\setminus\mathcal{F}_{2}
, and
C_{1}\notin\Adj(V_{2},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
, and
C_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}\setminus\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
, and
C_{2}\notin\Adj(V_{k-1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
.

Corollary 56
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be an essential ancestral graph and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=(\mathbf{V,E^{\prime}})
be an ancestral partial mixed graph such that
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have the same skeleton, the same set of minimal collider paths, and every invariant edge mark in
\mathcal{G}
is identical in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Suppose furthermore that edge orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
,
R2
,
R4
.
R8
,
R10
-
R12
. If there is an edge
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
such that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
would be implied by
Wang-R13
, then
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
would also be implied by
R13
.

By Lemma
65
, orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R9
. Furthermore, since
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}
contain the same minimal collider paths and since orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are closed under
R3
(Corollary
11
), the orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are also closed under
R3
. The Corollary then holds by Lemma
57
and Corollary
55
.

Lemma 57
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be an essential ancestral graph and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=(\mathbf{V,E^{\prime}})
be an ancestral partial mixed graph such that
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have the same skeleton, the same set of minimal collider paths, and every invariant edge mark in
\mathcal{G}
is identical in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Suppose furthermore that edge orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
,
R2
,
R8
,
R11
,
R12
. Let
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
be an edge in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and let
\mathbf{S}_{A}=\{V\in\mathbf{V}:V\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}A\text{ is in
}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\}\cup\{A\}
. Suppose that there is also an unbridged path
\langle V_{1},\dots,V_{k}\rangle,k>1,
relative to
\mathbf{S}_{A}
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
such that for every
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
there is an unshielded path
p_{i}=\langle W_{i_{1}}=A,W_{i_{2}}=B,\dots,W_{i_{m}}=V_{i}\rangle,{{m}\geq 3}
with no edge
W_{i_{j}}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}W_{i_{j+1}}
,
j\in\{1,\dots,m-1\}
on
p_{i}
.
Let
C_{1},C_{2}\in\mathbf{S}_{A}
be nodes such that
C_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}
, and
C_{1}\notin\Adj(V_{2},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
, and
C_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{k}
, and
C_{2}\notin\Adj(V_{k-1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
. Then the following hold:

(i)

C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
and
C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

(ii)

For every
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
,
p_{i}
is a possibly directed path from
A
to
V_{i}
.

(iii)

A\notin\cup_{i=1}^{k}\Adj(\{V_{1},V_{k}\},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
which also implies that
A\notin\{C_{1},C_{2}\}
.

(iv)

C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

(v)

V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}\leftrightarrow C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

(vi)

A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
and
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
are in
\mathcal{G}
.

(i)

Note that since
C_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}
it follows that
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
or
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. However, since
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
and since
C_{1}\notin\Adj(V_{2},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
, it follows that
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
cannot be in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
(otherwise, orientations are not closed under
R1
). Therefore,
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.
We can obtain that
C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
using analogous reasoning.

(ii)

Follows by Lemma
58
.

(iii)

We will only prove that
A\notin\Adj(V_{1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
by contradiction. The proof of
A\notin\Adj(V_{k}\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
would be exactly symmetric. Hence, suppose for a contradiction that
A\in\Adj(V_{1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
.

Note that since
p_{1}
is a possibly directed unshielded path (by
(ii)
above), we have that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
,
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
or
A\to V_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. We first show that having
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
in
\mathcal{G}
already leads to a contradiction.
Since
p_{1}=\langle W_{1_{1}}=A,B,\dots,W_{1_{m}}=V_{1}\rangle
is a possibly directed unshielded path from
A
to
V_{1}
and
\langle A,V_{1}\rangle
is in
\mathcal{G}
, it must be that
m\geq 4
i.e.,
p_{1}
must contain at least four nodes.
Moreover, if
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}
, then Lemma
37
implies that
p_{1}
must be a circle path in
\mathcal{G}
. Together,
p_{1}
and
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
contradict Lemma
39
.
Therefore,
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
is not in
\mathcal{G}
, and so
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
is also not in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Note that by Definition
53
and Theorem
54
, it is technically possible to have
A\equiv C_{1}
, but in this case we cannot have
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
, or
A\to V_{1}
, by
(ii)
. Lastly, consider that
A\neq C_{1}
and that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
or
A\to V_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and also that the corresponding edge in
\mathcal{G}
is
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
or
A\to V_{1}
.

Note that since
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}A
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, having
A\to V_{1}
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
(or in
\mathcal{G}
) would contradict that orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are clsoed under
R2
. Therefore, the only remaining option is that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}
.

Then since
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}
, we have that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{2}
is also in
\mathcal{G}
by Lemma
40
.
Note also that we know that
C_{1}\notin\Adj(V_{2},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
and that
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}A
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Therefore, either
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}A\leftrightarrow V_{2}
is already an unshielded collider in
\mathcal{G}
, or
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}A\to V_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.
In the former case, we now obtain a contradiction with
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
being in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
having the same minimal collider paths as
\mathcal{G}
. This is because
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
also contains
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
and unshielded collider
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}A\leftrightarrow V_{2}
and
C_{1}\notin\Adj(V_{2},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
. Since
\mathcal{G}
would need to contain the same unshielded collider and since orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are closed under
R3
(Corollary
11
), we obtain the contradiction.
In the latter case, we obtain a contradiction with
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
being in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
being closed under
R11
, since now we have that
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}A\to V_{2}
,
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
,
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
, and
C_{1}\notin\Adj(V_{2},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
.
This concludes deriving the contradiction in the case
A\in\Adj(V_{1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
.

(iv)

We will prove that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{1}
must be in
\mathcal{G}
and a symmetric argument can be used to show
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.
Note that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, so that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{1}
or
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{1}
must be in
\mathcal{G}
. We will assume that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and show that leads to a contradiction.

Since
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, and
p_{1}=\langle W_{1_{1}}=A,B,\dots,V_{1}=W_{1_{m}}\rangle
,
m>2
is an unshielded possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, we also have that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
is in
\mathcal{G}
and that the corresponding path in
\mathcal{G}
,
\langle W_{1_{1}}=A,B,\dots,V_{1}=W_{1_{m}}\rangle
is also unshielded and possibly directed. Note that
\langle W_{1_{1}}=A,B,\dots,V_{1}=W_{1_{m}}\rangle
is either a circle path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, or there is an arrowhead at some
W_{1_{l}},l\geq 2
, on edge
\langle W_{1_{l-1}},W_{1_{l}}\rangle
after which
W_{1_{l}}\to\dots\to W_{1_{m}},
by
R1
.
Therefore, by iterative application of Lemma
40
, we have that
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}B
is in
\mathcal{G}
, and also that
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}W_{1_{j}}
is also in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, for every
1\leq j\leq l
(in the case where
\langle W_{1_{1}}=A,B,\dots,V_{1}=W_{1_{m}}\rangle
is a circle path
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}W_{1_{j}}
for all
1\leq j\leq m
).

Then
\langle W_{1_{1}}=A,B,\dots,V_{1}=W_{1_{m}}\rangle
cannot be a circle path otherwise
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
would be in
\mathcal{G}
and contradict case
(i)
above. Hence, there must be an
l<m
such that
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}W_{1_{l}}\to W_{1_{l+1}}\to\dots\to V_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}
and also in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. However, this path together with
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
now contradicts Corollary
63
.

(v)

By case
(iv)
we have that
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}
and by
C_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1}
,
C_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{2}
, we know that
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Since
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
does not contain any new unshielded colliders compared to
\mathcal{G}
, it must be that
C_{1}\in\Adj(C_{2},\mathcal{G}).
We also know that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.3,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.8,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
(and
\mathcal{G}
). Below, we first show by contradiction that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.3,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.8,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
is not in
\mathcal{G}
. We subsequently argue depending on the size of
k
that this implies that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}\leftrightarrow C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
must be in
\mathcal{G}
by invoking Lemma
65
.

Suppose for a contradiction that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.3,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.8,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. The presence of the path
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}C_{2}
in
\mathcal{G}
implies, by Lemma
38
, that edge
\langle C_{1},C_{2}\rangle
must be of the form
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}C_{2}
in
\mathcal{G}
. However, note that path
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}C_{2}
is unshielded in
\mathcal{G}
and that it contains at least four nodes. The presence of edge
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}C_{2}
then contradicts Lemma
39
.
Hence, we conclude that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}
, or
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

Suppose without loss of generality that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.
Suppose additionally, that
V_{k}\in\Adj(C_{1},\mathcal{G})
.
Note that, here, we consider
k>2
. Otherwise, we have a contradiction with
C_{1}\notin\Adj(V_{2},\mathcal{G})
.
Let
V_{t}
,
t\in\{2,\dots,k-1\}
be a node with the largest index
t
such that
V_{t}\notin\Adj(C_{1},\mathcal{G})
. This node surely exists, since
V_{2}\notin\Adj(C_{1},\mathcal{G})
. Then consider the edge
\langle V_{t+1},C_{1}\rangle
. Since
V_{t}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{t+1}
and
\langle V_{t+1},C_{1}\rangle
is in
\mathcal{G}
and since
V_{t}\notin\Adj(C_{1},\mathcal{G})
it follows by Lemma
40
that
\langle V_{t+1},C_{1}\rangle
is of one of these forms in
\mathcal{G}
,
V_{t+1}\to C_{1}
or
V_{t+1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}
. In either case, we have that by concatenating
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{t}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{t+1}
and
\langle V_{t+1},C_{1}\rangle
we obtain an unshielded possibly directed path from
V_{1}
to
C_{1}
in
\mathcal{G}
(Lemma
41
) such that
V_{2}\notin\Adj(C_{1},\mathcal{G})
. Since additionally, we have that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}
, we obtain a contradiction with Lemma
65
.

Otherwise,
C_{1}\notin\Adj(V_{k},\mathcal{G})
(in this case it is possible that
k=2
), in which case
\langle C_{1},C_{2},V_{k}\rangle
is of one of the following forms in
\mathcal{G}
(Lemma
40
),
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
,
C_{1}\leftarrow C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
,
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
. In the latter two cases, similarly to above, we obtain a contradiction with Lemma
65
, because
V_{2}\notin\Adj(C_{1},\mathcal{G})
and by concatenating
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
and
\langle V_{k},C_{2},C_{1}\rangle
we obtain an unshielded possibly directed path from
V_{1}
to
C_{1}
(Lemma
41
) in addition to edge
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}
.

We now have that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
, so in order to show that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}\leftrightarrow C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
we only need to rule out that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
and
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}\to C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

Suppose for a contradiction that either
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
and
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}\to C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. In the former case we have that
V_{1}\in\Adj(C_{2},\mathcal{G})
(Lemma
40
), while in the latter case its possible that
V_{1}\notin\Adj(C_{2},\mathcal{G})
. In either case let
V_{s}
,
s\in\{1,\dots k-1\}
be the node with the smallest index that is not adjacent to
C_{2}
in
\mathcal{G}
. Such a node surely exists since
V_{k-1}\notin\Adj(C_{2},\mathcal{G})
.
Then since
V_{s-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{s}
and
\langle V_{s-1},C_{2}\rangle
are in
\mathcal{G}
and
V_{s}\notin\Adj(C_{2},\mathcal{G})
, Lemma
40
lets us conclude that
V_{s-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}C_{2}
or
V_{s-1}\to C_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. Therefore, similarly to above we now have that
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}
and
V_{k-1}\notin\Adj(C_{2},\mathcal{G})
and concatenating
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{s}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{s-1}
and
\langle V_{s-1},C_{2}\rangle
(Lemma
41
) yields an unshielded possibly directed path from
V_{k}
to
C_{2}
in
\mathcal{G}
which contradicts Lemma
65
.

(vi)

We have shown in case
(v)
that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}
and
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{2}
are in
\mathcal{G}
and thus, these are also in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
by
(i)
. Similarly, we have shown in case
(iv)
that
C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}A
and
C_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}A
are in
\mathcal{G}
, and by case
(iii)
we have that
A\notin\Adj(V_{1},\mathcal{G})

and
A\notin\Adj(V_{k},\mathcal{G})
. Therefore, Lemma
40
leads us to conclude that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
is an unshielded collider in
\mathcal{G}
and so is
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
.

D.1.2
On Possibly Directed and Unshielded paths

This subsection contains a few results on possibly directed paths, unshielded paths, and path concatenations. The main result of this section are Lemmas
58
and
59
, and the relevant proof structure is given in Figure
14
.

.

Lemma 58

(Possibly Directed Status of an Unshielded Path)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be an essential ancestral graph and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=(\mathbf{V,E^{\prime}})
be an ancestral partial mixed graph such that
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have the same skeleton, the same set of minimal collider paths, and every invariant edge mark in
\mathcal{G}
is identical in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Suppose furthermore that edge orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under

R1
,
R2
,
R8
,
R11
,
R12
.
If
p=\langle P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\rangle
,
k\geq 1
is an unshielded path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
such that there is no edge of the form
P_{i}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{i+1}
, then
p
is a possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Since
p
is an unshielded path and orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
it follows that if there is an arrowhead at
P_{i}
on any edge
P_{i}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{i+1}
, then
p(P_{i+1},P_{k})
. must be a directed path. Furthermore, Lemma
62
implies
p(P_{1},P_{i+1})
is an unshielded possibly directed path. Now, lastly, we have by Lemma
59
that
p(P_{1},P_{i+1})\oplus p(P_{i+1},P_{k})
is an unshielded possibly directed path.

Lemma 59

(Possibly Directed Path Concatenation)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be an essential ancestral graph and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=(\mathbf{V,E^{\prime}})
be an ancestral partial mixed graph such that
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have the same skeleton, the same set of minimal collider paths, and every invariant edge mark in
\mathcal{G}
is identical in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Suppose furthermore that edge orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under

R1
,
R2
,
R8
,
R11
,
R12
. If
p=\langle P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\rangle
,
k\geq 1
is an unshielded possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
q=\langle P_{k},\dots,P_{k+r}\rangle,r\geq 1
is a directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, then
p\oplus q
is a possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Follows by iterative applications of Lemma
60
and Corollary
42
.

Lemma 60

(Towards Possibly Directed Path Concatenation)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be an essential ancestral graph and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=(\mathbf{V,E^{\prime}})
be an ancestral partial mixed graph such that
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have the same skeleton, the same set of minimal collider paths, and every invariant edge mark in
\mathcal{G}
is identical in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Suppose furthermore that edge orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under

R1
,
R2
,
R8
,
R11
,
R12
. If
p=\langle P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\rangle
,
k\geq 1
is an unshielded possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and if
P_{k}\to P_{k+1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, then
p\oplus\langle P_{k},P_{k+1}\rangle
is a possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

It is enough to show that
P_{i}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
,
i\in\{1,\dots,k-1\rangle
is not in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

This claim holds for
i=k-1
since otherwise,
P_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{k+1}\leftarrow P_{k}
and the fact that
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is ancestral and that orientations are closed under
R2
would imply that
P_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{k}
is on
p
. And this fact would in turn contradict that
p
is possibly directed from
P_{1}
to
P_{k}
.

Hence, suppose for a contradiction that
P_{i}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
for some
i\in\{1,\dots,k-2\}
, and let
P_{j}
be the closest such node to
P_{k}
on
p
. Furthermore, note that
P_{j}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
is not in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, as
P_{j}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k+1}\leftarrow P_{k}
and the fact that orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
implies that
P_{k}
and
P_{j}
are adjacent. Further,
R2
implies that
P_{j}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
thus contradicting the assumption that
p
is a possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Then
P_{j}\leftarrow P_{k+1}
or
P_{j}\leftrightarrow P_{k+1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is an ancestral graph, we can also conclude that
p(P_{j},P_{k})
is not a directed path from
P_{j}
to
P_{k}
. Furthermore, since orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
and since
p
is an unshielded path, this also implies that
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{j}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{j+1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
by Corollary
43
.

Now, let
P_{l}
be the closest node to
P_{1}
on
p
such that
P_{l}\to\dots\to P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and if no such node is in
p
, then let
P_{l}\equiv P_{k}
.
Consider paths
P_{j}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{l}
and
q=p(P_{l},P_{k})\oplus\langle P_{k},P_{k+1},P_{j}\rangle
, where by construction,
q
is of one of the following forms
P_{l}\to\dots\to P_{k+1}\to P_{j}
, or
P_{l}\to\dots\to P_{k+1}\leftrightarrow P_{j}
. If
l>j+1,
these two paths contradict Lemma
61
. If
l=j+1,
then since orientation in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
,
P_{k+1}\in\Adj(P_{l},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
and furthermore, Corollary
63
implies that
P_{l}\to P_{k+1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. But closure under
R2
implies that
P_{j}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{l}
contradicting our assumption that
p
is a possibly directed path.

Lemma 61
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be an essential ancestral graph and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=(\mathbf{V,E^{\prime}})
be an ancestral partial mixed graph such that
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have the same skeleton, the same set of minimal collider paths, and every invariant edge mark in
\mathcal{G}
is identical in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Suppose furthermore that edge orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
,
R2
,
R8
,
R11
,
R12
. Then there are no two paths
p=\langle V_{1},\dots,V_{i}\rangle,i>1
and
q=\langle V_{i},\dots,V_{k},V_{1}\rangle
,
k>i
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
such that
p
and
q
have the same endpoint nodes and are of the following forms:

(1)

p
is an unshielded path of the form
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}\dots\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{i-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{i}
, and

(2)

q
is one of the following forms

(i)

V_{i}\to\dots\to V_{k}\to V_{1}
, or

(ii)

V_{i}\to\dots\to V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
, or

(iii)

V_{i}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{i+1}\to\dots\to V_{k}\to V_{1}
, or

(iv)

V_{i}\to\dots\to V_{j}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{j+1}\to\dots\to V_{k}\to V_{1}
,
k>j>i
.

Suppose for a contradiction that there are two paths with the same endpoints that are of the forms as discussed in
(1)
and
(2)
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Choose among all such pairs in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
the paths
p
and
q
with endpoints
V_{1}
and
V_{i}
such that for any other pair of paths
p^{\prime}
and
q^{\prime}
with endpoints
V_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}
and
V_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}
and such that
p^{\prime}
is of the form
(1)
, and
q^{\prime}
is of the form
(2)
, the following hold:
either
|p|<|p^{\prime}|
and
|q|\leq|q^{\prime}|
, or
|p|=|p^{\prime}|
and
|q|\leq|q^{\prime}|.

By choice of
p
and
q,
there cannot be any subsequence of
q
that forms a path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, that is of one of the forms:
(2)
(2)(i)
-
(2)
(2)(iv)
. In conjunction with Corollary
63
, we then have that there cannot be any edge between any two non-consecutive nodes on
q
. Hence,
q
is an unshielded path.
This further implies that
V_{i}\notin\Adj(V_{1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}),
and hence,
i>2
on
p
.

Next, consider path
p
. By assumption
p
is an unshielded path and above we concluded that
|p|>1
. Additionally, by Lemma
62
, there is no edge of the form
V_{l}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{r}
,
1\leq l<r\leq i
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. By the same reasoning, there is also no edge of the form
V_{l}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{r}
, for
1\leq l<r\leq i-1
. Furthermore, by choice of
p
and
q
there also cannot be an edge
V_{l}\to V_{i}
, or
V_{l}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{i}
,
1\leq l<i
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Lastly, by choice of
p
there also cannot be an edge of the form
V_{l}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{r}
,
1\leq l<r\leq i
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Hence, not only is
p
unshielded, but similarly to
q
, there is no edge between any two non-consecutive nodes on
p.

Revisiting the fact that
q
is unshielded, together with the assumption that orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
, the
[formula]
[formula]
edge on
q
must be either
\to
, or (if
q
starts with
[formula]
as in case
(2)
(2)(iii)
, then
p
must end with
and we just redefine
p
to include the
[formula]
edge).
We now break the rest of the proof up into cases depending on the form of
q
.

(2)(i)

Since
V_{k}\to V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, and since orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
,
V_{k}\in\Adj(V_{2},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
. The edge
\langle V_{k},V_{2}\rangle
cannot be of the form
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{2}
as that contradicts the choice of
q
(this would fall under case
(2)(ii)
). It also cannot be of the form
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{2}
as that contradicts that orientations are closed under
R2
.

Hence,
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
must be in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.
Since we now have that
V_{k-1}\to V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, and since orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
,
\langle V_{k-1},V_{2}\rangle
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. By the same reasoning as above, we now have that
V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
must be in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. However, now
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
contains the unshielded triples
V_{k-1}\to V_{k}\to V_{1}
and
V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
and edge
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
which contradicts that orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R11
.

(2)(ii)

Since we already discussed the case when
q
is a directed path in
(2)(i)
, we will assume that
V_{k}\leftrightarrow V_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Furthermore, since orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R12
, we know that
|q|>2
, that is,
k>i+1
.

Since
V_{k}\leftrightarrow V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, and since orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
,
V_{k}\in\Adj(V_{2},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
. Note, furthermore, that the edge
\langle V_{k},V_{2}\rangle
cannot be of the form
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{2}
as that contradicts the choice of
q
. Additionally,
V_{k}\leftarrow V_{2}
contradicts that orientations are closed under
R2
, since in this case
V_{2}\to V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{1}
and
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
would be in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Thus,
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
or
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
are in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.
Let us first consider the case where
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.
Now have that
V_{k-1}\to V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, so that since orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
,
\langle V_{k-1},V_{2}\rangle
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Note that
V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{2}
contradicts that orientations are closed under
R2
, and
V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{2}
contradicts the choice of
q
. Hence
V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. But now, consider the unshielded collider
V_{k-1}\to V_{k}\leftrightarrow V_{1}
,
V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
and
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
. By assumption
V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{1}
,
V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{1}
and
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
are also in
\mathcal{G}
, which contradicts that orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are closed under
R3
(Corollary
11
).

Hence, it is left to consider the case when
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.
Consider that
p(V_{2},V_{i})
is a possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and that
q(V_{i},V_{k})
is a directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and moreover, that there cannot be any edge
V_{l}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{r}
,
2\leq l<r\leq k
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
as that contradicts either that
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is ancestral, or the choice of
p
and
q.
Hence
t=p(V_{2},V_{i})\oplus q(V_{i},V_{k})
is a possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Note that if there is any edge
\langle V_{l},V_{r}\rangle
,
2\leq l<i<r\leq k
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, by choice of
p
and
q
, this edge cannot be of the form
V_{l}\to V_{r}
, or
V_{l}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{r}
.
Hence, any such edge must be of the form
V_{l}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{r}
.

Furthermore, consider any edge
\langle V_{l},V_{k}\rangle

2\leq l<i
. Then since
V_{1}\leftrightarrow V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
V_{1}\notin\Adj(V_{l},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
and since orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
, we can conclude that the
\bullet
on edge
V_{l}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{k}
must be an arrowhead, that is
V_{l}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
. Now, let
V_{s},s\in\{2,\dots,i-1\}
be the closest node to
V_{i}
on
t
such that
V_{s}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Consider again that any edge
\langle V_{l},V_{r}\rangle
,
2\leq l<i<r\leq k
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
must be of the form
V_{l}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{r}
.
Since orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R12
and
V_{k-1}\to V_{k}\leftrightarrow V_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
V_{1}\notin\Adj(V_{k-1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
this implies that we cannot have an edge
\langle V_{2},V_{k-1}\rangle
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Moreover, if
i>3
, then since
p(V_{1},V_{3})
is an unshielded path of the form
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{3}
and since
V_{1},V_{2}\notin\Adj(V_{k-1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
, we also cannot have an edge
\langle V_{3},V_{k-1}\rangle
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. We can apply the same reasoning to conclude that
V_{2},\dots,V_{i-1}\notin\Adj(V_{k-1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
.
Hence, also
V_{s},V_{s+1},\dots,V_{i-1}\notin\Adj(V_{k-1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
.

Now we have that
V_{s}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
,
V_{s},\dots V_{i-1}\notin\Adj(V_{k-1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
, and
V_{s+1},\dots,V_{i}\notin\Adj(V_{k},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
. Additionally,
t(V_{s},V_{k})
is of the form,
V_{s}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{i-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{i}\to\dots\to V_{k-1}\to V_{k}
and is a possibly directed path. Now we can choose nodes
V_{a}
and

such that
V_{a}
is on
t(V_{s},V_{i})
,

, and
V_{b}
is on

,

, and
V_{a}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{b}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
(
a=i-1
and

is a valid choice, so such pairs

exist). Moreover, we can choose
V_{a},V_{b}
such that
w=t(V_{s},V_{a})\oplus\langle V_{a},V_{b}\rangle\oplus t(V_{b},V_{k})
is an unshielded possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Then note that
V_{s}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
is also in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and that
V_{s+1}\notin\Adj(V_{k},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
by choice of
s
, which contradicts Lemma
65
.

(2)(iii)
,
(2)(iv)

Since
V_{k}\to V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and since orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
,
V_{k}\in\Adj(V_{2},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
. As in the proof of case
(2)(i)
, the edge
\langle V_{k},V_{2}\rangle
must be of the form
V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Now,
V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{2}
and orientations in

being closed under

imply that edge
\langle V_{k-1},V_{2}\rangle
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Furthermore, as
q
is unshielded, we know that
V_{k-1}\notin\Adj(V_{1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
. Putting it all together, we now have that
V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.3,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.8,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}\to V_{1}
,
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
, and
V_{k-1}\notin\Adj(V_{1},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
, which contradicts that orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R11
.

Lemma 62
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be an essential ancestral graph and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=(\mathbf{V,E^{\prime}})
be an ancestral partial mixed graph such that
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have the same skeleton, the same set of minimal collider paths, and every invariant edge mark in
\mathcal{G}
is identical in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Suppose furthermore that edge orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R2
,
R12
.
Suppose furthermore that there is an unshielded path
q=\langle V_{1},V_{2},\dots,V_{k}\rangle,k\geq 3
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
of the form
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{k}
. Then there is no edge
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{k}
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

If
k=3
, then since
q
is unshielded,
V_{1}\notin\Adj(V_{3},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
. For the rest of the proof, suppose that
k>3
and let
q^{*}
be the path in
\mathcal{G}
that corresponds to
q
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Additionally, suppose for a contradiction that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Consider the case where
V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.
Since
q^{*}
is of the form
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
,
k>3
in
\mathcal{G}
, the edge
\langle V_{1},V_{k}\rangle
is of the form
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{k}
in
\mathcal{G}
by Lemma
38
. But now this contradicts Lemma
39
in
\mathcal{G}
.
Since
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have the same skeleton we reach a contradiction.

For the rest of the proof, we consider the case where
V_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
, and therefore also in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. By Lemma
41
, path
q^{*}
is an unshielded possibly directed path from
V_{1}
to
V_{k}
in
\mathcal{G}
. Further, it also ends with an arrowhead pointing to
V_{k}
. Hence, Lemma
37
implies that edge
\langle V_{1},V_{k}\rangle
in
\mathcal{G}
is of the form
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
, or
V_{1}\to V_{k}
. Since
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}V_{k}
is supposed to be in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, we now conclude that
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
must be in
\mathcal{G},
which further implies that
V_{1}\leftrightarrow V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Furthermore, since
q^{*}
is an unshielded possibly directed path from
V_{1}
to
V_{k}
in
\mathcal{G}
(Lemma
41
), and
k>3,
and since
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
by Lemma
65
, it follows that
\langle V_{2},V_{k}\rangle
is in
\mathcal{G}
. If
k=4,
we now reach a contradiction with
q
being an unshielded path. Otherwise,
k>4
, and by Lemma
37
edge
\langle V_{2},V_{k}\rangle
is of the form
V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
, or
V_{2}\to V_{k}
in
\mathcal{G}
. Note that,
V_{2}\to V_{k}
cannot be in
\mathcal{G}
, otherwise
V_{2}\to V_{k}\leftrightarrow V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
which contradicts that orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R2
. Hence,
V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

Now, similarly to above, consider that
q^{*}(V_{2},V_{k})
is an unshielded possibly directed path from
V_{2}
to
V_{k}
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, and that
k>4
, and that
V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. Hence, it follows by Lemma
65
that
\langle V_{3},V_{k}\rangle
is in
\mathcal{G}
. If
k=5,
we now reach a contradiction with
q
being an unshielded path. Otherwise,
k>5
, and by Lemma
37
edge
\langle V_{3},V_{k}\rangle
is of the form
V_{3}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
, or
V_{3}\to V_{k}
in
\mathcal{G}
. Note that,
V_{3}\to V_{k}
cannot be in
\mathcal{G}
, otherwise
V_{3}\to V_{k}\leftrightarrow V_{1}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
V_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}V_{3}
is an unshielded path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
which contradicts that orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R12
. Hence,
V_{3}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

Note that the above argument can be repeated to conclude that
V_{4}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k},\dots,V_{k-2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}V_{k}
are all in
\mathcal{G}
, which ultimately leads to a contradiction with the assumption that
q
is an unshielded path.

Corollary 63
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be an essential ancestral graph and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=(\mathbf{V,E^{\prime}})
be an ancestral partial mixed graph such that
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have the same skeleton, the same set of minimal collider paths, and every invariant edge mark in
\mathcal{G}
is identical in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Suppose furthermore that edge orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are closed under
R1
,
R2
,
R8
. Suppose that there is a path
p=\langle P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\rangle,

k\geq 3
and edge
\langle P_{1},P_{k}\rangle
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Then the following hold

(i)

If
p
is a directed path from
P_{1}
to
P_{k}
, then
P_{1}\to P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

(ii)

If
P_{i}\to P_{i+1}
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,k-1\}\setminus\{j\},1\leq j\leq k-1
and
P_{j}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{j+1}
, then
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Follows by Lemmas
64
and
65
.

Lemma 64

(Maintaining the Ancestral Property)
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
is an ancestral partial mixed graph with orientations closed under
R1
,
R2
,
R8
,
R9
. Suppose that there is a path
p=\langle P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\rangle,

k\geq 3
and edge
\langle P_{1},P_{k}\rangle
in
\mathcal{G}
. Then the following hold

(i)

If
p
is a directed path from
P_{1}
to
P_{k}
, then
P_{1}\to P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

(ii)

If
P_{i}\to P_{i+1}
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,k-1\}\setminus\{j\},1\leq j\leq k-1
and
P_{j}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{j+1}
, then
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

We prove the two statements by induction on the length of
p
. For the base case of the induction
k=3
, and we have that both cases
(i)
and
(ii)
hold because

\mathcal{G}
is an ancestral partial mixed graph and because orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are closed under
R2
and
R8
.
Next, we show the induction step in each of the two cases.

(i)

Suppose that claim
(i)
holds for all paths
p^{\prime}
of length
n\leq k
, where
k\geq 3
.
Let
p
be a directed path with
k+1
nodes,
p=\langle P_{1},\dots,P_{k+1}\rangle
such that the edge
\langle P_{1},P_{k+1}\rangle
is also in
\mathcal{G}
. Let
p^{\prime}=\langle P_{1}=Q_{1},\dots,Q_{m}=P_{k+1}\rangle,m>1
be a shortest subsequence of
p
that forms a directed path from
P_{1}
to
P_{k+1}
in
\mathcal{G}
. If
m\leq k
, then
P_{1}\to P_{k+1}
is in
\mathcal{G}
by the induction assumption.
Otherwise
m>k
, that is
m=k+1
and
p^{\prime}\equiv p
, meaning that
p
is an unshielded path in
\mathcal{G}
.
Since
\mathcal{G}
is ancestral, this edge cannot be
P_{1}\leftarrow P_{k+1}
or
P_{1}\leftrightarrow P_{k+1}
.
Below we argue by contradiction that edge
\langle P_{1},P_{k+1}\rangle
cannot be
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
or
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
in
\mathcal{G}.

Suppose for a contradiction that
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. Since
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k+1}\leftarrow P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
, and since orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are closed under
R1
it follows that
P_{k}\in\Adj(P_{1},\mathcal{G})
. Hence, by the induction assumption,
P_{1}\to P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. But this further implies that
P_{1}\to P_{k}\to P_{k+1}
which is a subsequence of
p
that is a directed path is in
\mathcal{G}
, and that contradicts that
p^{\prime}\equiv p
.

Next, suppose for a contradiction that
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. Note that since
P_{1}\to\dots\to P_{k}\to P_{k+1}
is an unshielded directed path in the ancestral graph
\mathcal{G}
and since edge mark orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are closed under
R9
, it follows that
P_{2}\in\Adj(P_{k+1},\mathcal{G})
. Since
P_{2}\in\Adj(P_{k+1},\mathcal{G})
and
P_{2}\to\dots\to P_{k+1}
is in
\mathcal{G}
, by the induction assumption,
P_{2}\to P_{k+1}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. But now
P_{1}\to P_{2}\to P_{k+1}
is a subsequence of
p
that is a directed path is in
\mathcal{G}
. This contradicts that
p^{\prime}\equiv p
.

(ii)

Suppose that claim
(ii)
holds for all paths
p^{\prime}
of length
n\leq k
, where
k\geq 3
.
Let
p
be a path with
k+1
nodes,
p=\langle P_{1},\dots,P_{k+1}\rangle
such that
P_{j}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{j+1}
, for some
j\in\{1,\dots,k\}
, but
P_{i}\to P_{i+1}
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}\setminus\{j\}
and also such that the edge
\langle P_{1},P_{k+1}\rangle
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

Since
\mathcal{G}
is ancestral, the edge
\langle P_{1},P_{k+1}\rangle
cannot be of the form
P_{1}\leftarrow P_{k+1}
. Hence, for the claim
(ii)
, it is enough to show that this edge is also not of the form
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
in
\mathcal{G}
.

Suppose for a contradiction that
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. Since
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k+1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
and since orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are closed under
R1
it follows that
P_{k}\in\Adj(P_{1},\mathcal{G})
. If
p(P_{1},P_{k})
is a directed path, then by
(i)
above, we have that
P_{1}\to P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. But then
P_{1}\to P_{k}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
together with
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
contradicts that orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are closed under
R2
. Otherwise,
p(P_{1},P_{k})
contains either
[formula]
or a
\leftrightarrow
edge, so by the induction step
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.
However, in this case
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{k}\to P_{k+1}
and
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k+1}
are in
\mathcal{G}
, which contradicts that orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are closed under
R2
.

Lemma 65
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=(\mathbf{V,E})
be an essential ancestral graph and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}=(\mathbf{V,E^{\prime}})
be an ancestral partial mixed graph such that
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have the same skeleton, the same set of minimal collider paths, and every invariant edge mark in
\mathcal{G}
is identical in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.
Suppose furthermore that the edge
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and that there is an unshielded possibly directed path
p
, from
A
to
C
,
p=\langle A=P_{1},P_{2},\dots,P_{k}=C\rangle
,
k>3
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Then
P_{2}\in\Adj(C,\mathcal{G})
.

Let
p^{*}
be the path in
\mathcal{G}
that corresponds to
p
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have same skeleton,
p^{*}
is an unshielded path in
\mathcal{G}
.
Furthermore, since
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
has additional edge orientations compared to
\mathcal{G}
, any possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
corresponds to a possibly directed path in
\mathcal{G}.

Therefore,
p^{*}
is a possibly directed unshielded path in
\mathcal{G}
.

Suppose first that
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}C
is in
\mathcal{G}
. Then
P_{2}\in\Adj(C,\mathcal{G})\equiv\Adj(C,\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
because otherwise, orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are not closed under
R9
.
Next, suppose
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}C
is in
\mathcal{G}
. Then Lemma
37
and Corollary
43
together imply that
p^{*}
is an unshielded path of the form
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{2}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}\dots\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}C
in
\mathcal{G}
. Furthermore, since by assumption
|p^{*}|\geq 3
and since
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}C
we obtain a contradiction with Lemma Lemma
39
.

Appendix E
Supplement to Section
6

Completeness of orientations with respect to
R9
follows from Lemma
65
.
Completeness of orientations with respect to
R3
follows from the fact that we are adding consistent orientation knowledge to
\mathcal{G}
, which means we never elicit a new unshielded collider in
\mathcal{G}^{{}^{\prime}}
. For
R3
to be invoked a new unshielded collider would be needed.

Let
p_{\mathcal{G}}=\langle P_{1},P_{2},\dots,P_{k}\rangle
be a path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
that makes up a shortest directed or an almost directed cycle with edge
\langle P_{1},P_{k}\rangle
. If
k=3
, we are done.

Hence, suppose for a contradiction that
k>3
and let
p_{\mathcal{G}}
be the path in
\mathcal{G}
that corresponds to path
p_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
in
\mathcal{G}
.
Note that
p_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
must be an unshielded path since due to the completion of orientations in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
under
R2
and
R8
, any shield
\langle P_{i},P_{i+2}\rangle
would imply the existence of a shorter directed or almost directed cycle in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Therefore,
p_{\mathcal{G}}
is an unshielded path of length
k>3
. Hence, it cannot be a circle path (Lemma
39
).

By Corollary
43
, it follows that
P_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.
Using the same reasoning as in the previous paragraph, we can also conclude that
P_{2}\notin\Adj(P_{k},\mathcal{G})
and that
P_{1}\notin\Adj(P_{k-1},\mathcal{G})
.
Since orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are closed under
R9
, it therefore follows that we cannot have
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.2,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{k}
in
\mathcal{G}
.

Hence
P_{1}\leftarrow P_{k}
, or
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(4.3,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k}
, or
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. Since
P_{1}\notin\Adj(P_{k-1},\mathcal{G})
, and since
\mathcal{G}
is ancestral, Lemma
41
implies that
P_{1}\to P_{2}\dots\to P_{k}
cannot be in
\mathcal{G}
.
Hence
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{2}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

But now
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{2}
,
P_{2}\notin\Adj(P_{k},\mathcal{G})
, and Lemma
40
, imply that
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{k}
is not in
\mathcal{G}
. Hence,
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k}
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

But now
P_{1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.0}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}P_{k}
and the path
p_{\mathcal{G}}
from
P_{1}
to
P_{k}
that does not contain
P_{i}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}P_{i+1}
,
i\in\{1,\dots,k-1\}
and ends with
P_{k-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}P_{k}
contradict Lemma
37
.

Suppose for a contradiction that
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is not maximal, that is, there is a possibly inducing path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Then there is also a minimal collider path that is a possibly inducing path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. The corresponding path in
\mathcal{G}
must then also be a minimal collider path and a possibly inducing path.

Among all shortest possibly inducing paths that are minimal collider paths in
\mathcal{G}
choose a path that has the shortest distance to its endpoints (Definition
46
). Let this path be
p=\langle A,Q_{1},\dots Q_{k},B\rangle,k>1
. Then
Q_{i}\in\PossAn(\{A,B\},\mathcal{G})
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
and there is at least one
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
such that
Q_{i}\notin\An(\{A,B\},\mathcal{G})
(otherwise
p
is an inducing path). Note that
\mathcal{G}
cannot contain inducing paths as it is an essential ancestral graph and there is at least one MAG it represents
[
Zhang, 2008b
]
.

Let
Q_{j}
,
j\in\{1,\dots,k\}
, be the closest node to
A
on
p
, such that
Q_{j}\notin\An(\{A,B\},\mathcal{G})
and suppose without loss of generality that
Q_{j}\in\PossAn(B,\mathcal{G})
. Hence, let
q=\langle Q_{j}=Q_{j,1},Q_{j,2},\dots,Q_{j,k_{j}}=B\rangle,k_{1}\geq 2
be a shortest possibly directed path from
Q_{j}
to
B
in
\mathcal{G}
. By Corollary
42
,
q
is then an unshielded possibly directed path. Hence, by Lemma
47
(ii) on path
p
,
k_{j}>2
(otherwise,
Q_{j}\in\An(B,\mathcal{G})
). Furthermore, by Corollary
43
,
q
must start with edge
Q_{j}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(1.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\put(1.5,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(2.9,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}Q_{j,2}
in
\mathcal{G}
.

Now, by Lemma
45
, either
B\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}Q_{j,2}
or there is some
Q_{j_{+}}
,
j_{+}\in\{j+1,\dots,k\}
, such that
Q_{j_{+}}\leftrightarrow Q_{j,2}
. We cannot have
B\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}Q_{j,2}
as that contradicts
q
being an unshielded possible directed path from
Q_{j}
to
B
. Similarly, either
A\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}Q_{j,2}
or there is some
Q_{j_{-}}
,
j_{-}\in\{1,\dots,j-1\}
, such that
Q_{j_{-}}\leftrightarrow Q_{j,2}
. In the former case, consider the path
p_{1}=\langle A,Q_{j,2},Q_{j_{+}}\rangle\oplus p(Q_{j_{+}},B)
and in the latter case, consider the path
p_{2}=p(A,Q_{j_{-}})\oplus\langle Q_{j_{-}},Q_{j,2},Q_{j_{+}}\rangle\oplus p(Q_{j_{+}},B)
. Either way, we have now obtained either a shorter minimal collider path than
p
that is a possibly inducing path in
\mathcal{G}
, or one that is of the same length but with a shorter distance to its endpoints, which is a contradiction.

We consider both directions below.

\Leftarrow
:

If conditions
(i)
and
(ii)
are satisfied, then Lemma
4
and the fact that
R4
subsumes
Zhao-R4
immediately allow us to conclude that
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
have identical minimal collider paths.

\Rightarrow
:

Suppose that every minimal collider path
p_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}=\langle V_{1},\dots,V_{k}\rangle,k>1
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
corresponds to a minimal collider path
p_{\mathcal{G}}=\langle V_{1},\dots,V_{k}\rangle,k>1
in
\mathcal{G}
. We need to show that this implies
(i)
and
(ii)
hold.

Since every unshielded collider is a minimal collider path, the unshielded colliders in
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
must be identical. Hence,
(i)
holds. Furthermore, every discriminating collider path
q_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}=\langle A,Q_{1},\dots,Q_{m},B\rangle,m\geq 2
in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
that is also a minimal collider path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, will definitely satisfy
(ii)
in
\mathcal{G}
.

Lastly, suppose that
q_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}=\langle A,Q_{1},\dots,Q_{m},B\rangle,m\geq 2
is a discriminating collider path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, but not a minimal collider path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}.
Then there must be a subsequence
q_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}^{\prime}
of
q_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
that is a minimal collider path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Furthermore, note that since
Q_{i}\to B
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime},
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,m-1\}
, and
A\notin\Adj(B,\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
the subsequence of
q_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}^{\prime}
that forms a minimal collider path in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
must contain
Q_{m}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
.
Therefore,
Q_{m}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
is in
\mathcal{G}
.

Now, note that
Q_{m-1}\leftarrow Q_{m}
cannot be in
\mathcal{G}
since we know that all invariant orientations in
\mathcal{G}
are also in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and we also know that
Q_{m-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}Q_{m}
is in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Therefore, either
Q_{m-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}Q_{m}

Q_{m-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}Q_{m}
is in
\mathcal{G}
. We can now rule out that
Q_{m-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}Q_{m}
is in
\mathcal{G}
as
Q_{m-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.1,1.0){\line{1}{0}{2.4}}\put(4.0,1.0){\circle{1.0}}\end{picture}Q_{m}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
and Lemma
40
would imply that
Q_{m-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
is in
\mathcal{G}
and therefore in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
contradicting our assumption that
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
contains

.
Therefore,
Q_{m-1}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\bullet$}\put(1.0,0.0){$\rightarrow$}\end{picture}Q_{m}\begin{picture}(0.0,0.0)\put(0.2,0.0){$\leftarrow$}\put(3.0,0.0){$\bullet$}\end{picture}B
is in
\mathcal{G}
.
